I’ve been skipping actual meals for days and mostly getting by on coffee and protein bars.\n\nIt didn’t feel like a big deal at first, but then I noticed my clothes were hanging looser, so… I figured I should probably come in and talk to someone. Is that the kind of thing you need to know?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I didn’t weigh myself right away, so I can’t give you an exact number, but my clothes started fitting looser, like noticeably.\n\nI guess I’d stopped really paying attention to meals—kind of let the tracking go, was mostly just coffee and protein bars for a few days at a time. I know that’s probably not ideal. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I wouldn’t be thrilled if I suddenly gained a lot, I guess, but I’m not sitting there scared of it or anything.\n\nIt’s more like I just haven’t been keeping up with eating properly. I don’t want to make it sound like some big weight thing if it isn’t. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently too high?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think it’s too high. I mean… I don’t love thinking about it too much, but I wouldn’t say I see myself as overweight or anything.\n\nIt’s more that I just haven’t been eating normally lately, not that I’m trying to get my weight down. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight. Not in that way.\n\nI mean, I guess nobody wants their weight to swing a lot, but I’m not avoiding food because I’m scared I’ll gain. It’s more like I just haven’t been on top of eating. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten to the point where you felt like you couldn't stop?\n\nPatient: Not really… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I ate more than I probably needed, like anyone does, but not where I felt out of control or like I couldn’t stop.\n\nAt least, nothing that stands out to me that way. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that you might lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really worry about losing control when I’m eating. I mean, I might get distracted or eat kind of randomly sometimes, but not like I’m afraid I won’t be able to stop.\n\nAt least that’s not something I’ve noticed. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling less interested in food lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I didn’t really think of it as “less interested” at first, more like I just wasn’t paying attention to it.\n\nI stopped keeping track of meals like I usually would, and I’d go days without really having a proper meal—mostly coffee and protein bars, that kind of thing. And then I noticed my clothes were fitting looser… or hanging off more than usual, honestly.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I just didn’t feel that hungry or motivated to deal with food, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel hungry when you skip meals?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I probably should, honestly. Like, I’d skip actual meals and just have coffee or maybe a protein bar, and I wouldn’t really feel hungry in the normal way… or I’d ignore it.\n\nI know that doesn’t sound great. It kind of got away from me—I stopped paying attention to food tracking and then suddenly my clothes were loose. But no, I wasn’t really feeling hungry enough to make myself eat. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of interest in food lately due to not feeling hungry or is there another reason for it?\n\nPatient: I think it was mostly not feeling hungry, but also… I just stopped caring about it, if that makes sense. I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic—it just sort of got away from me. I was given this temporary PMO-level responsibility at work, and I guess I wanted to handle it well, you know, not make a big deal out of it.\n\nThe eating stuff kind of built up over maybe three weeks after that. I don’t want to blame it all on work, but the timing lines up. I stopped caring about tracking my food, skipped actual meals for days, and was basically getting by on coffee and protein bars. I mean… I don’t love thinking about it too much, but I wouldn’t say I see myself as overweight or anything.\n\nIt’s more that I just haven’t been eating normally lately, not that I’m trying to get my weight down. Not in that way.\n\nI mean, I guess nobody wants their weight to swing a lot, but I’m not avoiding food because I’m scared I’ll gain. It’s more like I just haven’t been on top of eating. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten to the point where you felt like you couldn't stop?\n\nPatient: Not really… I don’t think so. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that you might lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really worry about losing control when I’m eating. I mean, I might get distracted or eat kind of randomly sometimes, but not like I’m afraid I won’t be able to stop.\n\nAt least that’s not something I’ve noticed. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling less interested in food lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. And then I noticed my clothes were fitting looser… or hanging off more than usual, honestly.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I just didn’t feel that hungry or motivated to deal with food, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel hungry when you skip meals?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I probably should, honestly. Like, I’d skip actual meals and just have coffee or maybe a protein bar, and I wouldn’t really feel hungry in the normal way… or I’d ignore it.\n\nI know that doesn’t sound great. It kind of got away from me—I stopped paying attention to food tracking and then suddenly my clothes were loose. But no, I wasn’t really feeling hungry enough to make myself eat. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of interest in food lately due to not feeling hungry or is there another reason for it?\n\nPatient: I think it was mostly not feeling hungry, but also… I just stopped caring about it, if that makes sense. Like the food tracking I usually do kind of fell apart, and then I was going days without real meals, just coffee and protein bars.\n\nI didn’t really notice how off it had gotten until my clothes started hanging loose. I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic—it just sort of got away from me. Is that the kind of reason you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has anything been stressing you or going on in your life that you think might be connected to your change in eating habits?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there probably was something connected. I was given this temporary PMO-level responsibility at work, and I guess I wanted to handle it well, you know, not make a big deal out of it.\n\nThe eating stuff kind of built up over maybe three weeks after that. I don’t want to blame it all on work, but the timing lines up. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding food for any reason other than just not feeling hungry?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was avoiding food on purpose, exactly. It wasn’t like I was scared of eating or trying to do anything extreme. It was more that I just… stopped caring about it. My food tracking went out the window, and then I was skipping real meals for days and kind of living on coffee and protein bars.\n\nI honestly didn’t even realize how bad it had gotten until my clothes started feeling loose. So, I guess it was more than just not feeling hungry, but not avoiding food for a specific reason, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your change in eating habits is related to your emotional state?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it probably is related. I don’t want to overstate it, but when I’m in that kind of headspace, I just stop paying attention to basic stuff.\n\nThat’s honestly part of why I came in—my eating went completely off the rails. I stopped caring about tracking my food, skipped actual meals for days, and was basically getting by on coffee and protein bars. Then my clothes started hanging loose before I even really registered it.\n\nSo… yes, I think my emotional state had something to do with it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you gained a little weight?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I don’t think gaining a little weight would be the main thing that changes it, honestly.\n\nThe bigger issue is that my eating got really disorganized—I stopped caring about tracking food, skipped real meals for days, and was mostly getting by on coffee and protein bars. But I don’t think the weight itself is what I was focused on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would start eating normally again if you weren't worried about your weight at all?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, maybe yes, but I don’t think weight was really the thing driving it.\n\nIt was more like my eating just got completely disorganized. I stopped caring about tracking anything, and then I was skipping actual meals for days and mostly just having coffee and protein bars. I didn’t really notice how far off it had gotten until my clothes started hanging loose.\n\nSo if I wasn’t worried about weight at all, I think I’d still need help getting back into a normal routine. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think it was really fear of gaining weight, no… at least not mostly.

It was more like my eating just kind of fell apart. I stopped caring about tracking what I was eating, and then I was skipping real meals for days and just getting by on coffee and protein bars. I didn’t even really notice how bad it had gotten until my clothes started hanging loose.

Yeah… I think there probably is. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but something was definitely off in my head around it.

Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding food for any reason other than just not feeling hungry?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was avoiding food on purpose, exactly.
